**AssignCredentials before Resume.** Admissible on a fresh replacement entry: §4.7.1 rule 4 lists `AssignCredentials` among entry-creating requests, and `Resume` then resolves the same-token entry under rule 7; CODE-3's S11 order leaves the call for CODE-4 item 20.3 to insert at S14 ([spec.37.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.35.review-applicability.1]).
**Driver pacing.** The pre-claim retry is `now + backoff + jitter` with `--resume-driver-backoff-seconds` (default 10), so SPEC-1b item 1's "attempts again later" is not an unbounded retry; the SPEC-1g pre-claim check runs at most once per backoff ([spec.41.review-reliability.1], [spec.31.review-performance.1]).
**Terminal binding reclamation (D6).** Design **Terminal binding reclamation.** is the one home of the trigger, the release-or-evict rule, the publish sites, and the rejected alternatives (CODE-5 item 2d, CODE-4 item 27). `input_required` was already adopted before CODE-5 item 1. Do not refile the cross-replica terminal leak per publish site.

### Traps

**Do not restore the old SPEC-1b condition, `UpdatedAt` as guard or token, the post-exit bump, or the deleted SPEC-1b claims.** State plus generation is ABA-prone; `AdvanceLastSeq` moves `UpdatedAt`.
**Terminal writers read `fromState` outside their mutator.** `handleTransition` and `handleDelete` read it on the locked row (CODE-6 item 1), and `expireSession`, `failSession`, `failFinalizing`, `cascadeToChildren`, and `cancelSubtree` read it there under D7 (non-spec Design **Terminal transition.**); other terminal writers remain a §7.2 step 4 defect owned elsewhere.
**Binding stamp.** `resume_rebind.go` stamped `G−1`; CODE-4 item 5.4 restamps with `G`.
**Do not add a `resuming` guard to §10.1.2 step 1.** SPEC-2a's step-0 tie and SPEC-1b exits carry exclusivity; the step-1 0-row path covers the race order.
**Fence-restatement sweeps.** Grep with lines joined; "must have received" hides from "must receive". No §28 site may describe how a podless session acquires the holder role; §28.6 **One holder per session.** is the home, and its "`CH-FENCE` establishes the constraint" stays.
**"No bound pod" is read from state.** Recovering rows name the failed pod; fixtures must carry it.
**Do not describe a pointer's target in SPEC-1f.**
**Replaced: inbox buffering, off-holder forward, `DrainForResume`, `clearInboxOnResume`, drain after commit.** Each lost to duplicate or stranded delivery; do not restore retract-after-drain, expiry in the delivery loop, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the durable inbox `EXPIRE`, or a backlog resume keyed on an interrupt suspension's inbox.
**Release migration is not the forbidden entry migration.** The trap forbids migrating on entry to `suspended`; the release migrates after the clear. Do not move it to the `running → suspended` write.
**Replaced: migrate before the clear.** A held-pod resume's `deliverBuffered` mid-`Send` would deliver twice. Clear first.
**Accepted migration windows.** A crash loses the in-memory inbox, and a failed post-migration re-read on a terminal session leaves migrated entries with no reclaimer (SPEC-5z item 11). Refile only with a widening path.
**Replaced: synchronous terminal re-check release.** The CODE-4 item 5 re-check row called `releaseExecutor` itself, which reached only this replica's binding and missed every binding held on another replica. D6 (`ReleaseTerminalBinding`, CODE-4 item 27, triggered by CODE-5 item 2d) replaced it; do not restore a per-site synchronous release or a by-name reclaim in the funnel ([redesign1.apply.6]).
**No at-least-once sentence in SPEC-5i.** Do not cite `delivered_message_ids`.
**Never route a refused held entry by path 6 or re-enqueue it.**
**Read failure versus removal failure.** A `LREM`/`ZREM` error stays a returned error; only `Peek`/`PeekOldest` errors return nil. CODE-6 item 7 gets no read-error branch. Under a read failure a new message can overtake older held entries; that ordering loss is accepted.
**Delivery lock.** Do not take it in `SuspendIdle` or `ReleaseSuspendedPod`, do not hold it together with `slotAccountLocks`, and do not call `ResumeSuspended` holding it.
**Replaced: idle atomicity from `TurnInFlight` alone, `TryLock` in `SuspendIdle`.**
**Replaced: every turn-state mechanism (redesign 13).** The session-row turn boundary (`turn_open`, `turn_seq`, `CloseTurn`, `TurnInFlight`), the adapter turn count, its checkpoint carriage (`CheckpointSummary`/`checkpoint_manifest`/`ResumeRequest` `turn_count`), and the unknown-count state are all deleted. Migration number 0184 now belongs to CODE-7's counted index. Idleness is the gateway's `maxClientIdleSeconds` clock together with the adapter's activity window, which counts frames, platform and connector calls in flight, and attributed `busy` (Decision 16). Do not reintroduce a turn count, a count carriage, a turn record, or a gateway-side turn tracker.
Record runtime-side activity at the hub tap on the runtime output's single reader, never in the `Attach` send loop; `linefanout.Hub` drops lines read with no subscriber. Keep the seal check and the activity read in one hold of `s.mu`. Any `SealSession` error or refusal leaves the session `running`.
**Do not attribute a bare ack's `busy` to any session.** On a concurrent pod one busy session would keep every co-tenant from idle suspension. A bare ack answers liveness for every monitor (CODE-6 item 18.3), which is a separate behavior.
**Do not reintroduce a gateway `Attach` opening, `OpenStream`, or an `Attach`-scoped heartbeat for idle suspension (OD-S8).** Monitors start only in `markSessionStartWritten`; `writeSessionEnd` stops and joins one before its write, and `deregisterSlotLocked` cancels one. No heartbeat follows a session's `session_end`, on the `DemoteSDK` path included.
**Do not refuse a seal for want of `busy`.** With no attributable `busy`, the seal rests on frames and platform and connector calls alone (Decision 30). The only refusals are an unwritten `session_start` and an untapped runtime (SPEC-5ab **Session seal.**).
**Do not call `SealSession` for a `type: mcp` or service-mode session.** `sweepIdle` expires it through `IdleSuspendable` (CODE-6 item 5).
**Do not add `UnsealSession` on a not-sealed answer.** It reopens the single-lifter rule; it needs an owner decision.
**MISTAKE: "Next idle firing completes or lifts."** An earlier round staged in SPEC-5ab item 2 "While a sealed session is `running`, the next idle firing ... seals it again and either completes the suspension ... or lifts the seal." That contradicted the not-sealed answer (the seal stays in place) and SPEC-5f's write-nothing-else rule for a not-sealed answer: a sealed session with continuing activity reaches neither outcome.
The sentence is deleted; the remaining bounds are "a later attempt lifts it or the entry is removed" plus `maxSessionAge` (Settled **Not-sealed keeps the seal.**). Do not make the sentence true by adding `UnsealSession` on a not-sealed answer ([spec.17.fix-G2.1]).
**Connector attribution cost of option B.** Earlier readings listed a new session-attribution rule as a cost of OD-I3 B. That was wrong: `connectorToolProvider.Call` already resolves the calling session through `callingSession()`, as the platform provider does. Do not re-add the cost to any comparison ([f7.apply-OD-I3]).
**Do not reintroduce "not evaluated" for elicitation waits** in SPEC-5e, SPEC-5p, or the watchdog; `idleClockRunningStates` stays `{running}`.
**Do not cite §28.5.3 **Addressing.** for the activity tap.** That rule resolves only against the delivering stream; the tap resolves as `deliverToSession` does.
**A §29.3 row that points at the `awaiting_client_action` row for a `suspended` transition drops resume admission.** It must also cite §11.1 **Resume admission.**
**Frame-identifier red window.** `TestFrameIdentifierAgreesAcrossSchemaSpecAndReference` is red from S5 until the S8 schema step, so TEST-0's tier-11 run at S7 meets it red ([reconcile.deliverables.5]). Do not refile.
**Clear-then-release order.** SPEC-5b step 2 and SPEC-5f clear `pod_assignment` before the release; orphan GC predicate 1 reclaims a crash between them, and the same window exists after every terminal write. Not a finding.
**Replaced: the read-only `AttachTurnState` query, the `StampSync` admission and its fail-open window, and the delivery lock held across the release (redesign 9).** A stale-row delivery to a sealed session is refused by the adapter and held. Do not re-add a gateway admission write, a per-replica marker, or a second turn query at the release.
**Replaced: `SuspendIdle` step 3's branches keyed on an earlier store error, its "counts as landed" predicate, the sentinel `errIdleSuspendSuperseded`, and the unseal on any guard miss (redesign 1).**
They lost because the branch taken depended on the attempt's history rather than on the locked row, the post-error row-form list had no single rule, and the clean-miss unseal fired on a generation miss, where the session already has a newer coordinator or resume incarnation.
`classifyIdleWrite(seen, row, gen)` on the locked row decides every try; Stale (generation other than `bind.CoordinationGeneration`) sends the pod no RPC, evicts the own binding, and logs `coordinator_preempted`. The pod release is not generation-checked, so this conjunct is the only guard against a stale coordinator releasing an adopter's pod.
Do not restore a post-error branch, a `SuspendedAt` identity, a new column, or a `CoordinatorFence` rule that ends the seal; extend the classifier and `TestClassifyIdleWrite_spec_6_2` instead. Who lifts the seal and how an unlifted seal ends have one home, SPEC-5ab **Session seal.**. SPEC-5f, CODE-6 item 4 step 3, CODE-6 item 18, and the edge-case bullets cite it and restate none of it. Do not restore "another attempt lifts only after a takeover": a later attempt on the same replica re-seals and may lift.
**Replaced: the adapter as the single judge of idleness, with a `busy` MUST over every frameless piece of work ([mechanism.idle-decision-whole.1]).**
It lost because its premise was false: the adapter serves `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` and blocks in `platformToolProvider.Call` for each call, and every gateway qualifying event already defers the `SealSession` attempt. The `busy` MUST was later widened to any work by the owner (OD-I1 B), and the §15.4.6 row and check were rewritten to match; the single-judge design stays rejected.
Idle suspension requires both the gateway's `maxClientIdleSeconds` clock and the adapter's activity window to find the session idle. The adapter counts frames, attributed `busy`, and `CH-MCP-PLATFORM` and `CH-MCP-CONNECTOR` calls in flight (`beginToolCall`, CODE-6 item 18.1), and a proxy-mode stream stamps the gateway clock once per relayed chunk (CODE-6 item 18.1, row G4).
Do not delete the SPEC-5e agent-work, `lenny/await_children`, or proxy-mode clauses, do not narrow `busy` back to the handling of a `message`, and do not resolve a tool call and count it in separate holds of `s.mu`.
**Replaced: 0091's `renewedGeneration` and unconditional `RecordHandoff`.**
**Replaced: a restore that assigns no credential lease.** Keep `AssignCredentials` after `handshake` and before `cl.Resume`.
**Replaced: the held elicitation resolution (answer B to OD-N4).** The `wait_ended` column and its `interactions` migration, `EndWait`, `HoldElicitationResolution`, `sessioninbox.Message.SenderKind`, and the `elicit_<id>` held message are deleted. Do not restore a hold, a `from.kind: system` delivery of user text, an expiry keyed on `interactions.session_id`, an `expired` phase, a release-path expiry before step 4's pod release or outside its `slotAccountLocks` hold, or a clause in the §29.3 interaction-resolution row. Answer B lost because a pod failure before the gateway noticed the dead stream lost the resolution, the §8.2 rule it cited holds the elicitation rather than its resolution, and it labelled client text as platform text. Also rejected with it: an `inReplyTo` field (no `inReplyTo` reaches the runtime), a durable reclaim with an `enqueued` column (it cannot tell a live child's wait from an ended one), and answer A limited to the idle release ([redesign1.apply.2], [spec.30.fix-design-G3.1], [redesign1.apply.3]).
**Replaced: `ErrOnClaimed`.** The retry loops stop on an unwrapped hook error.
**Do not add a release inside the shared `connect`.** The Resume slot path releases through `releaseResumeSlot`, never `DeleteClaim`.
**Replaced: the session-wide release on `Bind` failures.**
**Replaced: the lock as lease protection.** The generation scope (CODE-4 item 24) protects a resume's leases; the lock only keeps the replacement binding from `releaseExecutor`. Do not delete the lock or narrow it to slot bindings.
**Replaced: the OD-C3 residual.** Do not return a session-wide `ReleaseSession` from `Release` or `ReleaseSlot`.
**Lease generation value.** Do not write "the session's `coordination_generation` at its assignment"; finalize leases record 0 while the row is at baseline 1.
**Replaced: sandbox-name identity in step 4.** Same-pod slots and re-claimed recycled pods reuse the name; use pointer identity.
**Replaced: the `OnReleased` observer.** It missed a release between mint and track, a `ReleaseSession` snapshot before the replacement, and cross-replica releases. Do not reintroduce an in-process observer.
**Renewal-stop rules.** A nil `w.leases` counts as no record; tests with a nil store must pass a store holding the lease. Do not simplify `Advance` back to `Put` (upsert resurrects released rows). The old `scanLease` folds errors into not-found, but `Lookup` (CODE-4 item 23.2) returns a scan, KMS, or decode error as an error, so a Postgres failover retries renewal rather than stopping it; only `pgx.ErrNoRows` is a missing record ([non-spec.6.review-performance.1], [spec.23.review-security.1]). pgstore `Advance` must move the `expires_at` column. A missing record also follows §11.4 user revocation.
**Replaced: per-site `errHoldReread`.** Use `ReadPrimary`; no `store.Get` read decides a write.
**Replaced: nil-returning declining mutators.** Return a sentinel.
**Guards against a stale listed row.** Compare with the listed row's `PodAssignment` inside the mutator; any write that clears `PodAssignment` and then releases needs a guard in every list-then-write reconciler. Memstore tests cannot see lag; use a stale-`Get` wrapper. The message-path stale-read test must use snapshot podless `suspended` over stored `running`.
**Interrupt guard.** Do not guard on `running` alone; `EndpointInterrupt` admits `input_required`.
**Replaced: per-replica periodic backlog evaluation and the OD-P3 residual.** Do not move the evaluation back onto every watchdog tick; any sentence granting the leader exclusivity must name the condition (the watchdog lifetime sweep runs on every replica). Do not add a SPEC-2a sentence naming the leader evaluation.
**Do not re-add the list of podless resume mechanisms.** CODE-6 items 4 step 3a, 7, 16 are code homes; keep the edge-case label **Held message whose `resume_pending` write does not follow.**
**Replaced: on-demand adoption and lease renewal of podless rows.** Do not re-add an adoption or forward for a podless row; `Upsert` clears `released_at`, so a one-shot mirror release is undone.
**Replaced: migration 0183 with `ListFilter` fields.** 0183 is now the partial index only.
**Index-only migration test.** It must be named under tests/tier2_component/migrations/ with no `columns`; lint-migrations pass 3 fails otherwise.
**`adoptedState` pod conjunct.** A binderless `/start` commits `running` with no pod and no lease. If SPEC-2a's set changes again, cut CODE-5 to a pointer rather than editing both.
**MISTAKE: "reachable only after a bind".** Redesign 5 justified the exclusion that way; binderless `running` rows exist, and the claim would have failed 0060's test.
**Binderless versus no lease store.** "Dev mode" meant no lease store while `holdsPod` means no binder; they diverge in Compose Mode. Check any gate against binderless rows. Fixtures without a binder make `holdsPod` true (`newReportFixture`), so no-op guard tests pass vacuously.
**MISTAKE: renaming a path-6 action in one place.** Grep the whole proposal.
**MISTAKE: recording a regression the proposal causes as a pre-existing defect.** Fix with a code carrier (budget-key TTL, client-cycled root, renewal re-mint, readopt binding).
**MISTAKE: crediting 0091 with a lease keep-and-renew change.**
**MISTAKE: lettered RECORDS-1 cross-references lag.** 7(f) inbox fallback, 7(g) barrier generation, 7(h) `inbox_cleared`, 7(i) crash duplicate, 7(j) durable trimmer, 7(k) nil-returning mutators, 7(l) unindexed listings, 7(m) rebuild bound, 7(n) credential lookup fail-open, 7(o) §10.4 reattach buffer, 7(p) tenant-deletion queue erasure, 7(q) minimal-state resume, 7(r) client-message activity stamp.
**MISTAKE: the `Refuted: store-outage degradation` entry over-reached.** It refuted the admission write's Postgres dependency, which was a real conflict with §12.3; the Redis peek stall was a confirmed finding. The refutation stands only for DLQ message loss under Redis loss.
**MISTAKE: a "fixed" history entry with two halves.** The drain-failure label landed while the Description did not; check every half before trusting a fixed list.
**MISTAKE: `scanTree` and the client-cycled-root acceptance.** Both were withdrawn; the cycle is closed by re-arming on `enterResumePending`.
**MISTAKE: line citations into the proposal's own files.** Deleting a row shifted `spec-changes.md` lines and falsified summary citations. Cite by heading or bold label.
**Glob trap.** `*spec-changes.md` matches `*non-spec-changes.md` in `sed -n`, `grep`, and `git diff --` (including `git diff -- proposals/0092*/*spec-changes.md`); use the full name. RECORDS-1 sub-items are written "   - (b) ..." under item 7, so grep "- (b)", never "7(b)" ([non-spec.45.review-plan-conformance.1]). A Bash command naming `spec/` paths that writes files is blocked by the spec-lease hook.
**Anchor-check false misses.** A whitespace-collapsing checker misses anchors with runs of spaces (YAML comments, state blocks): grep those with `-F`. `grep -F` on a pattern with escaped backticks, and an unquoted `$session_id`, give false misses. `grep -i SSA` matches "meSSAge"; use `-w`. Retry before reporting an anchor missing ([spec-recheck.1.review-applicability.1], [spec.21.review-citations.1], [spec.23.review-citations.1]). The §28.5.3 **Addressing.** anchor "which carry no per-session identifier" and the CH-CHECKPOINT/CH-BARRIER anchors are hard-wrapped, so a single-line grep misses them; the SPEC-5ac item 8 anchor sits at spec/15:2378 after the Go block, still under the **SDK Handler types.** label, and is unique (re-judged immaterial nine times; do not refile) ([spec.41.review-citations.1], [spec.41.review-applicability.1]).
**Recheck delta source.** Take a recheck's delta from `git diff` on the proposal directory; a scratchpad snapshot can predate the whole loop and give a noise diff ([non-spec-recheck.1.review-citations.1]).
**Budget-key "fails closed" is a spec property only.**
**`RearmTree` rules.** Post-commit, best-effort, never inside a mutator; no SCAN; `ParentSessionID` is overloaded by derive, and listing by effective root covers it.
**New edges out of `suspended`.** Add each to CODE-6 item 14's exit-site list.
**Typed nil.** `budgetKeyRearmer` returns untyped nil.
**Interrupt routing.** One registration; fakes return the REST body; `session_control_test.go` needs retargeting under the blanket rule.
**Wrong releases.** `ReclaimClaimed`, `Release`, `ReleaseSlot`, `rollbackBinding` (pre-scope) and `failResume` with a foreign token; on a slot pool `DeleteClaim` deletes a shared claim.
**Lease acquire.** No acquire inside `releaseEarlierBinding` or `releaseThenResumeOnPod`.
**`onClaimed` signature.** Added with CODE-3 (S11); callers pass nil until CODE-4 (S14).
**`recordRouteRejection`.** Nil writer panics.
**Do not add PreMessageDelivery to the REST path.**
**Deadline safety.** Keep step 2's guard and item 3.4's sentence.
**Never treat a non-guard `Update` error in `completeResume` as a guard miss.**
**Post-commit tail lives in one table.** CODE-4 item 5 **Post-commit tail.** replaces the per-site clauses of items 5.3, 5.4, and 5.5, which twice recorded a double audit row and left relinquish and above-`G` without `session.resumed`, `children_reattached`, or held delivery. Change a cell, never a step. The bus, audit, cleanup, and tree-recovery rows stay on the resumer on every landed column, because an adopter cannot tell an owed resume tail from a crash takeover and would duplicate `children_reattached` (§7.2); do not move them to the adopter or add a durable tail marker. Only `deliverBuffered` moves, to CODE-4 item 26 through the CODE-5 item 2c hook, on the spec basis SPEC-5i and SPEC-5i2 stage (the adoption is a delivery point), and the session-server half stays in CODE-4 because CODE-5 lands first. A relinquished or superseded resumer that publishes an event does not violate §10.1.5. Classification precedes the partial-manifest cleanup. The `resumeExit` mapping stays in the item 25 table only.
**Landed-test traps.** Keep both conditions; do not import `podHeld`; no "recovery state" exclusion list.
**Commit repeat pacing.** Refuted on materiality at least nine times (scratchpad/cp-log histories); do not refile.
**Coordinating-replica detail.** Do not add a details key.
**`holdsPod` placement.** CODE-4 item 8.
**Off-holder `deliverBuffered`.** Fails in `streamFor`; not a defect.
**Gate tests use a held-pod row.**
**`HeldLen` test row.** Interrupt row. The `Idle` arguments are pinned by `TestCoordinatorHeldLenCountsInboxAndDLQ`, the `SuspendIdle` tail case, and `TestBacklogRecheckerResumesPodlessHeld_spec_7_2`.
**Replaced: per-outcome enumerations in hold tests.**
**Nil-on-miss idiom.** Any notifying watchdog write needs a sentinel.
**Watchdog release test.** Do not rewrite it to wait.
**`expiry_counter_test.go` rewrite.** Seed `SuspendedAt`.
**Tier-11 probe.** CODE-5 rewrites the `isRunningPod` probe.
**Tier-11 off-holder matrix test.** Red from S2 until TEST-0 at S7, which lands before the S8 schema step so that the schema step's tier-11 run meets it green; spec-lane steps run no tiers. Do not refile.
**Lane rules.** Spec-lane steps run no tiers; do not refile the S5 to S7 window.
**validate-maps and lint-migrations.**
**Generated files.** Never hand-edit values.schema.json or manifests.yaml.
**Summary file lists.** Mirror every Targets edit; when a fix adds a file to a deliverable, update the heading, files-touched list, and index together, and re-check the step's tier list.
**CODE-3 tests.** Only CODE-3 symbols.
**Docs targets on session-lifecycle.md.** Grep for `suspend`; check every clock a sentence names.
**Banned docs phrases.** Tier-11 bans the retired phrases, including "Pod failure while suspended (pod still held)" without backticks, and requires the retries-exhausted edges.
**Envelope pointer count.** Seven links in spec/07.
**Refuted: the 7-day suspended population as a finding.** The caps do not bound it (Settled **Leader capacity.**). Podless rows get no lease renewal and no mirror upsert, but the Sweeper still calls `leases.Get` per non-terminal row on every replica every 15 s (coordination.go:322), and `sweepSuspended` lists `suspended` rows every watchdog tick. Both are 7(l)-class costs that predate 0092, the leader pass is indexed, paged, and bounded to 32 concurrent `HeldLen` calls (refile only if paging or that bound is removed, [non-spec.25.fix-design-G2.1]), and the cap COUNTs read the 0184 index. Refile only with a new code path. Do not refile the §15.7 SDK `busy` sentence, the §15.1 messages-precondition `resuming` omission (internal state), or the bare-ack runtimes.
**Refuted: store-outage degradation.** Message loss only; see the MISTAKE above.
**Refuted: expiry against delivery, clock skew, GC races.**

**Refuted: docs-mirror findings.** A docs finding needs a wrong staged docs edit or a failing tier-11 test.
**Help text.** Per-pool `maxClientIdleSeconds` loosens the bound; help text only.
**Pre-existing, not filed.** `toResponse` returns `resuming` raw; watchdog expiries on a non-coordinator drain no inbox; docs state-machines.md:203 and concepts.md:80 drift; `PodSpiffeURI` has no production setter; `assignCredentials` fails on any provider; `credassign.Client.ReleaseSession` is replica-local; `LeasesBySession` swallows query errors; a step-4 release error leaves no Sweeper eviction; do not add a minimal-state branch to the driver (the gap is RECORDS-1 item 7(q)).
**`FailureExpiredIdle` doc comment.** Reused for the suspended lifetime and not rewritten.
**MISTAKE: the `resumeHeldPod` PreconditionError wrap (non-spec rounds 13 to 16).** A two-`%w` `PreconditionError` wrap moved CODE-4 to CODE-6 (a forward reference, because CODE-4 lands before CODE-6), back to CODE-4, and was then deleted because it answered 409 for a row raced to `awaiting_client_action`, which `/resume` admits. It cost three rounds.
Do not reintroduce a hand-built `PreconditionError` in `resumeHeldPod` in either item; `session.PreconditionError` has no `Unwrap`. Settled **Held-pod `/resume` race.** is the design.
Check the checklist step of every cross-reference a fix adds between CODE items, and land a test in the step that builds its behavior ([non-spec.14.review-applicability.1], Settled **Test landing rule.**).
**MISTAKE: a "take branch X" pointer.** Round 16 routed the held-pod fallback with "take the `awaiting_client_action` branch above", which passed the read `suspended` row to a callee that guards on its argument's state, so the guard missed. A pointer at a call site is not a specification when the callee guards on its argument; name the argument ([non-spec.17.fix-G1.1]).
**MISTAKE: SPEC-5y's step-10 half.** It was staged before redesign 1 restored idle expiry for `type: mcp` and service-mode sessions, so it dropped a live trigger and named the podless suspended-lifetime expiry, which never reaches §29.4 step 11. Do not re-add the lifetime to step 10 "for completeness"; §29.4 traces a session attached to a claimed pod ([spec.23.fix-G2.1]).
**Replaced: the `DeliverHeld` read-pool fallback, the message-path `StampSync` fail-open, and their tier-8 failover tests (non-spec rounds 3 to recheck 3).** Those rounds built a classification fallback, a `DeliverHeld` fallback that returned the fallback read's error, and `TestPrimaryFailoverDeliversToRunningSession_spec_7_2` around the `StampSync` admitter. Redesign 9 deleted the admitter, and Settled **Message-path read failures.** gives `DeliverHeld` the caller's row. Ledger text naming `StampSync`, `SetTurnAdmitter`, `ErrSessionSuspended`, or a `DeliverHeld` read is stale.
**`currentState: resuming` leak.** `writePreconditionError` copies `pe.CurrentState` raw, so any test asserting `currentState` on a `resuming` row pins a §15.1 violation; assert the code and the row instead ([non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]).
**`SuspendedAt` is never cleared.** Leaving `suspended` keeps the old value; pair any `SuspendedAt` predicate with a state check, and never use `SuspendedAt.IsZero()` ([non-spec.13.review-mechanism.1]).
**"Names no pod" versus `!holdsPod`.** They diverge with no binder wired. Spec text may say "names no pod"; non-spec text that describes a guard cites the item that implements it (CODE-4 item 9) ([non-spec.5.review-single-source.1]).
**Concurrent-pool isolation assertions.** A pod-wide seal leaves a co-tenant `running` while its messages go to the DLQ, so "keeps running" discriminates nothing; assert a delivered response ([non-spec.8.fix-design-G3.1]).
**`session_sealed:` prefix.** `envelopeWriteStatus` formats `what: err`; the seal refusal needs its own branch so the message begins `session_sealed:`, which the executor's prefix match needs ([non-spec.13.review-citations.1]).
**Untapped runtime in tests.** A test that builds the adapter gRPC server before assigning `Runtime` gets every `SealSession` refused as untapped ([non-spec.10.review-fresh.1]).
**DOCS-1b seal rows.** The SPEC-5ab rows say "(**Session seal.** below)", which has no target on adapter-contract.md; drop or replace the pointer when copying ([non-spec.11.review-docs-alignment.1]).
**Gap-fence wording.** §10.1.2 step 2 gap rule (b), "reset any transient ... state", could be read as resetting the seal; SPEC-5ab keeps the seal and the adapter resets nothing. Revisit only if a gap reset is ever built and counts the seal ([spec.13.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.8.review-security.2]).
**"Seal" collides with §7.1.** The workspace seal-and-export is a different mechanism; N3 covers channel identifiers only, so this is not a finding ([spec.12.review-edit-sites.1]).
**Delivery lock and a turn.** `PodExecutor.Send` has no per-turn timeout, so a held delivery holds the lock for a whole turn, bounded by heartbeat escalation; that is design D3, not an unbounded wait ([non-spec.16.review-reliability.1]).
**Tier 10.** Tier 10 tests runtime binaries through lenny-compliance and never pkg/adapter; the seal changes no CH-MSGSOCK contract, so no tier-10 case is owed ([non-spec.19.review-test-coverage.1]).
**Tier-8 Postgres fixtures.** `newStreamChaosGateway` hard-codes memstore and wires no tenant store; a pgstore case must seed `tenants` first and use UUID session ids, must not wire a tenant store (production answers 500 before classification), and injects either a failover proxy or a `blackholeProxy` partition (the partition case heals with `p.partitioned.Store(false)`, tests/tier8_chaos/stream_failure_chaos_test.go:390-394) ([non-spec.26.review-citations.1]) ([non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.6.follow-up-fix.1], [non-spec-recheck.1.review-citations.1]). The failover and partition tests pass only because the fixture wires no tenant store; keep that note in the test text, and never add a tenant-read fallback to make REST meet a wider I2 ([non-spec.20.fix-design-G3.1]).
**Resume chunk resolution errors.** `resolveResumeChunks` returns no grants on any resolver error, including a transient one, and `classifyResume` then degrades to `ResumeFull`, so the client is told nothing was lost. Keep "no manifest, dev mode, pre-chunked checkpoint" distinct from a transient error, which is a pre-claim failure under CODE-4 item 3.5 ([non-spec.13.review-reliability.1]).
**Before costing a per-row sweep call.** Check whether D2 (the local-binding check) returns first; otherwise the call runs once per replica per tick ([non-spec.16.review-performance.1]).
**SPEC-5ac item 2 stays a pointer.** When a later redesign changes activity, edit SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** only; do not re-add seal-predicate text (activity, "does not seal a session that reported busy", the case with no attributed busy) to SPEC-5ac item 2, and do not move the deleted "whatever other work the runtime is doing" trap into SPEC-5ac in other words, because SPEC-5ab's "Work a runtime begins after the session's last activity and before the seal without producing activity does not prevent the seal, and a release that follows the seal ends it." states it (wording since spec round 30, [spec.30.fix-design-G1.1]). Redesign 15 had to edit both homes; round 24 cut the second ([spec.24.fix-G1.1], [spec.24.fix-design-G1.1]).
The runtime-author DOCS bullet (non-spec-changes.md, the bullet beginning "`docs/runtime-author-guide/integration-levels.md`") is a licensed restatement of the predicate and must track SPEC-5ab, not SPEC-5ac. Settled **Adapter seal.**'s "do not restate either here" line named the sites (USEFUL).
**Per-chunk stamp in `flush`.** The proxy-mode per-chunk gateway stamp (CODE-6 item 18.1, row G4) belongs in the `flush` callback, because `RecordUsage` runs after the stream ends; the tree stamps a stream once, after `RelayStream` returns, so without the per-chunk stamp the SPEC-5e proxy-mode clause is false ([idle-decision-whole.apply.1], [mechanism.idle-decision-whole.1]).
**Seal then gateway timeout.** A `SealSession` that seals on the adapter and then exceeds the 5 s gateway deadline is the "does not answer" branch with a seal left in place; SPEC-5ab "A seal that its attempt does not lift stays" covers it. It is the settled **Not-sealed keeps the seal.** tradeoff; do not refile ([spec.24.review-mechanism.1]).
**Round-24 dropped alternatives.** Do not refile: a not-sealed answer overwriting a newer gateway stamp (a proxy chunk between listing and answer is lost for seconds, corrected at the next chunk); SPEC-5f's "ends no work the runtime reported" holding only while the heartbeat interval is shorter than the idle window (default 30 s against the 60 s playground floor; Settled **Heartbeat interval.**); SPEC-5t's exception list omitting the not-sealed answer (a not-sealed answer means the session was not idle under the conjunction, so the bound did not fire) ([spec.24.review-mechanism.1]).
**Pre-running DLQ TTL.** The Pre-running DLQ TTL against SPEC-5i's recovering-only enforcement is settled (archive review-log-archive.md:729, :733, :3418); do not refile it as a contradiction ([spec.24.review-single-source.1]).
**Replaced: the per-site increments of `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` in CODE-4 items 3.6, 3.7, 5.3, 5.4, and 5.5 and their counter assertions in the tier-1 driver tests (redesign 1 of 2026-10-11).**
They lost because the sites disagreed: **Landed at `G`.** ran step 4, whose relinquish branch ran step 5's success counter, and then ran step 5's success counter again, so a relinquished commit that landed after a store error counted `success` twice.
CODE-4 item 25 is the one mechanism: `attemptResume` defers one `recordResumeAttempt(row.PoolRef, exit)` after the item 3.1 read, `completeResume` returns a `resumeExit`, and the item 25 table is the closed list of exits and their outcomes. `TestResumeAttemptsCounterByDriverExit_spec_16_1` pins it, and SPEC-5q states the same population in terms of `resuming` incarnations.
Do not add a counter call at a commit, relinquish, report, or `handleResume` site, and do not count a hook store error, a shutdown exit, or the binderless commit; extend the item 25 table and its test instead ([mechanism.resume-attempts-counter.1]).

**Replaced: the CODE-4 item 5.3 Landed test and Exit bullets, the binderless "landed test keeps its store-error condition" clause, and the item 5.2 rule "repeat the `Update` until it succeeds or its guard fails" (redesign 1 of 2026-10-12).**
They lost because `Update` returns an empty row when its mutator returns an error, so the guard row existed only if the mutator copied it; the repeat never ended on a row that `DeleteByUser` or `DeleteByTenant` hard-deleted; and the binderless path carried a separate patched copy of the rule.
`classifyCommit(o commitObservation, b commitBaseline) commitOutcome` is the one mechanism: the mutator copies the locked row into `seen` first, a miss returns `errResumeCommitMissed`, the repeat ends on nil, that sentinel, or `ErrNotFound`, and the item 5.3 table maps the observation to a **Post-commit tail.** column. `TestClassifyCommit_spec_7_3` pins it.
Do not reintroduce a landed predicate at a call site, a repeat that ignores `ErrNotFound`, or a `CoordinationGeneration` read on the binderless path; extend the item 5.3 table and its test instead ([redesign1.apply.5]).
**Replaced: Decision 21's "No terminal writer cancels a running attempt on any replica" and the edge case "The attempt learns of it at its next write" (OD-T2 option A).**
It lost because §7.2 snapshot-close step 1 ("Cancel the in-flight restoration RPCs") is normative text the proposal leaves standing, and a terminal writer on the attempt's own replica can meet it with one cancel call; OD-3 accepted the divergence only for another replica.
CODE-4 item 2a is the one mechanism: the attempt registers its cancel function in the attempt record before the goroutine starts, `CancelResumeAttempt` is called by post-terminal hook step 1 (non-spec Design **Terminal transition.**) and by `podTerminateFanOut.terminateLocal`, and the binder releases its claim on `context.WithoutCancel` (CODE-3 item 3 **Detached release.**).
Do not bypass the hook, register the cancel inside the goroutine, or release a claim on the attempt context; a terminal write on another replica still reaches the attempt at its next write, and that remains accepted ([redesign1.apply.5]).
**Rejected: moving `recoverDelegationTree` to "Skipped" on the Relinquished and Above `G` columns, and a Decision 35 that enumerates the tail's effects (OD-H2 option B and the redesign's SPECIFICATION 2 split).**
`nodeNeedsRecovery` reads only the local pod registry, but under CODE-4 item 11 a non-holder's `ReattachNode` writes nothing and its `DriveResume` is refused by D1, so the split changes no behavior.
The staged **Post-commit tail.** cells stand, and Decision 35 stays a column citation as [non-spec.42.review-single-source.1] required.
Do not refile the non-holder tree recovery as a defect, and do not add an "each effect fires once per commit" claim; the binderless path can run the **Landed** tail twice by design (CODE-4 item 5.3 invariant) ([redesign1.apply.5]).
**The busy window has two ends.** SPEC-5ac item 8's window ends when the handler has returned AND the SDK has written its response. Ending on the response alone leaves a streaming, non-final turn busy until `session_end` (round 26); ending on handler return alone lets an idle ack precede the response (round 27). Do not "fix" the SDK to stay busy after a streaming non-final return, keep the pending count raised until a Final reply, or count `lenny/output` calls as activity ([spec.26.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.27.fix-design-G1.1]).
**MISTAKE: gating the eager subprocess reader on spawn origin.** Round 20 started `Serve` "only for a `Start`-spawned child", but `Send` reuses a Start-spawned child and scans the same pipe through `sess.stdout`, so a second reader would split its lines. The gate is the recorded tap. `TestSubprocessExecutorStartSpawnsProcess`, `TestSubprocessStartSpawnWritesNoSessionStart_spec_28_5_3`, and tier-3 `TestSubprocessSessionStartValidatesAgainstSchema_spec_28_5_3` run Start then Send untapped and must pass unchanged ([non-spec.21.fix-design-G1.1]).
**echo-concurrent unheld heartbeat.** A heartbeat for an unheld or ended session must never reach `route`: `rejectUnheld` drops non-message frames unanswered, and the adapter then reads the runtime as hung. The existing unaddressed-heartbeat tests stay valid. The tier-10 batteries build only echo, streaming-echo, and delegation-echo, so only the tier-1 test pins echo-concurrent, and only a direct `checkLevel` map assertion catches a missing entry (`buildProbeStub` and `allPass` iterate the map) ([non-spec.21.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.21.review-edit-sites.1]).
**MISTAKE: the failover edge case predated `admitResume`.** **Message during a §12.3 primary failover.** said a `suspended` target answers `inbox_unavailable` and labelled the seal-refused answer as the suspended-row answer. Under CODE-7 item 4 the primary count reads fail first (REST 500, MCP Go error). The bullet is now a pointer; do not write outcomes into it again ([non-spec.20.fix-G3.1]).
**Reader-facing activity text.** Any docs restatement of the idle window must carry SPEC-5ab's frame exclusions; "frames are activity" unqualified implies heartbeat traffic keeps a session running. Do not add a separate clause for a `busy: true` ack, which the report clause already covers. The DOCS-1b list still admits `session_started` and unaddressed frames, which is a docs-mirror non-finding unless a tier-11 test reads it ([non-spec.25.fix-design-G4.1], [non-spec.26.review-mechanism.1]).
**Never paraphrase "activity".** SPEC-5ab's bulleted inventory is the only definition, and paraphrasing it as "a frame or a platform or connector call" missed non-activity frames (round 30). Any rewrite of the no-activity-work sentence says "activity" and cites nothing else. Add an activity site as a CODE-6 item 18.1 table row, never as prose: the open-list form lost because sites were restated in four summary bullets and CODE-6 item 5 ([spec.30.fix-design-G1.1], [redesign1.apply.2]).
**The tap must not call `deliverToSession`.** The tap resolves the entry as `deliverToSession` does, but `deliverToSession` also increments `lenny_adapter_unaddressed_frame_rejected_total`, which §16.1 defines as once per Attach stream; reuse only its resolve rule ([non-spec.29.review-mechanism.1], [non-spec.29.review-fresh.1]).
**CH-RUNTIMEOPS observer tests.** A zero-token `llm_request_completed` is the discriminating case, because the token sink fires only on non-zero counts; a fixer who hangs the stamp on the sink passes a non-zero case. The CH-RUNTIMEOPS case of `TestActivityClockStampsEveryFrame_spec_4_7_1` must wire through `WireDirectModeUsage`: a direct `SetActivityObserver` call passes even when the install is missing ([non-spec.37.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.37.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Leader paging traps.** `id > ''` fails on pgstore's UUID column, and memstore's string compare hides it, so only the tier-2 case discriminates. The index must lead `(tenant_id, id)`; an index on `tenant_id` alone sorts the whole podless set per page. Do not loosen a page list error into a retry loop, and do not use `errgroup.WithContext` ([non-spec.25.fix-design-G2.1], [non-spec.26.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Do not harmonize `ResumeBacklog` errors.** It returns false on an `admitResume` count error and true on a `HeldLen` error; the count-error clause is the discriminating assertion ([non-spec.26.fix-design-G2.1]).
**New test and fixture files.** A new tier-2-or-higher test file needs a spec-map entry (only tests/tier4_integration is directory-globbed), and a new non-test Go file in a new tests/ dir needs a change-graph key; record both in the same edit ([non-spec.25.review-applicability.1], [non-spec.23.fix-design-G1.1]).
**External test and the adapter clock.** heartbeat_external_test.go is package `adapter_test` and cannot set the unexported `clock`; its "injected clock" needs an export_test.go setter or a move to the internal test. Not staged ([non-spec.25.fix-G1.1]).
**`credential_rejected` is non-retryable only by default.** Never write "non-retryable" or "spends no retry" unconditionally for it in staged text; a client `retryableFailures` naming it takes the retryable edge (`TestClassifyFailureCredentialRejected_spec_7_3`). Cite SPEC-1b item 3 for the mapping and SPEC-1g only for the predicate. `isCredentialRejection` is a positive list; classifying every `*CredentialAssignmentError` as a rejection would make pool exhaustion or a Token Service outage non-retryable ([spec.30.fix-design-G2.1], [spec.30.follow-up-fix.1], [redesign1.apply.2]).
**Entry routes need the Entry paths bullet.** Any route into `awaiting_client_action` a later section adds goes into §7.3 **Entry paths:** as a pointer with no count, and "In each case, client intervention is required." stays ([spec.31.fix-design-G1.1]).
**Failure-only paragraphs.** Do not cite §7.3 **External side effects across recovery.** or §8.2 **Pending elicitations:** for anything a suspension release triggers; both are failure-only. §8.2 **Replay on resume:** is trigger-neutral. Do not re-add a citation justifying the runtime's re-raise ([spec.31.fix-design-G2.1]).
**The §29.3 interaction-resolution row.** Do not re-add an enqueue clause or a "no call is blocked on a pod" premise in any form; the row covers tool-use approvals too, a child of a podless root can still be waiting and polls the store, and §9.2 item 6 is the home ([spec.30.fix-design-G3.1], [redesign1.apply.3]).
**Expiry key and the lock.** SPEC-5p2 expires by raising session, not by pod or generation. The release site runs under `slotAccountLocks` before any same-replica claim; the `ReportSessionFailure` site runs after `applyFailureFromActive` unlocks, which is safe only because a whole restore cannot fit inside one DELETE. If the expiry moves off the release lock or becomes asynchronous, scope the key to the departing incarnation ([spec.35.review-reliability.1], [non-spec.39.review-mechanism.1]).
**`awaiting_client_action` is not podless.** Do not write "holds no pod" or "no pod is bound" for it in any lease, clock, or adoption sentence ([spec.32.fix-design-G1.1]).
**"Attempt" names two populations.** SPEC-1b calls a pre-claim failure an attempt, while the counter counts observed `resuming` incarnations; any metric or audit row keyed on "resume attempt" says which. Phrase a counted failure as "observes the commit not take effect", never "releases its uncommitted pod", because the binder also releases after a hook store error, which stays uncounted ([spec.35.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.38.fix-design-G1.1]).
**MISTAKE: inventing owner glosses for Decision 21.** The summary once said the immediate cancel "holds only on the replica running the attempt", and round 40 widened the acceptance to "no cancel on any replica" and called it closed under delegation. Neither was the owner's: the OD-3 question text (c90f207c9) accepted only a terminal state written on another replica. The same-replica case went to the owner as OD-T2 and is now CODE-4 item 2a. Do not stage a §7.2 step 1 or §6.2:243 rewrite; OD-T1 settles that the cross-replica divergence stands ([non-spec.40.follow-up-fix.1], [f9.human-decisions.OD-T1], [redesign1.apply.5]).
**Do not attribute agent-written defaults to the owner.** "The owner's stated default" for OD-H2 B appeared only in agent text, with no owner statement in the repository; an entry says so rather than citing it as fact ([f9.human-decisions.OD-H2]).
**Terminal-writer lists exclude terminate.** `POST /terminate` never admits `resuming`; any list of terminal writers that land on an attempt names DELETE, parent cascade, admin cancel, `sweepMaxAge`, and the writers `TestTerminalWriterInventory_spec_7_2` lists ([non-spec.42.review-mechanism.1]).
**Echo-exception anchors.** "on the frames it emits in response, on every pod." appears twice in §28.5.3, so item 9 anchors on the longer "echoes the `sessionId` it was handed ..." phrase, and spec/28:625 stays unedited. Item 10 edits a table row, which stays one physical line ([spec.39.fix-design-G1.1], [spec.39.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Scope of "sends no RPC to the pod".** That reason for the resumer running the tail holds only for the bus, audit, store, and tree-recovery rows; the landed columns also hold `stampBindingGeneration(G)`, `fenceResumedPod` (an adapter RPC), `deliverBuffered`, and the connection close, so no summary site may widen it to "the rows" or "those columns" ([non-spec.42.follow-up-fix.1]).
**Planning records move between rounds.** The launching session edits BUILD-GAPS.md and the remediation plan between rounds (commit 1dd5c9469 rewrote the §10.2 entry and R19/R20); re-check every RECORDS-1 quoted anchor against `git log -1 -- gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md`, locate plan entries by name, and read R19/R20 as well as §10.2 ([non-spec.31.review-edit-sites.1], [non-spec.42.review-plan-conformance.1]).
**Near-copy pairs.** SPEC-5f's refused-message re-route and SPEC-5i **Synchronous delivery.**'s re-read overlap only on a seal refusal, where they agree; the SPEC-5d timer bullet names `maxSuspendedSessionSeconds`, and SPEC-5g is its home. When a round edits either half, re-check the pair rather than adding a third statement; the Design paragraph 1 route list is a summary, so do not add a third route list ([spec.38.review-single-source.1], [spec.41.review-single-source.1]).
**Sentinel single-source checks.** Before filing CODE-4 item 8 against CODE-6 items 6 and 7 for a sentinel branch, grep review-log-archive.md for the sentinel; `errHeldDeliveryFailed` was examined and rejected at archive:6820 ([non-spec.31.review-single-source.1]).
**MCP interceptor order.** The shipped MCP buffered branch enqueues before the PreMessageDelivery chain runs; CODE-6 item 7 **MCP** moves the chain ahead of Classify. A fix that drops that order reopens a content-policy bypass for held messages ([non-spec.45.review-security.1]).
**Forward stamp needs the guard.** The age arithmetic (row over-idle, adapter not idle) does not prove the not-sealed stamp forward, because a gateway-only qualifying event can land between the sweep's read and the write; the forward-only guard is what closes it ([spec.41.fix-design-G1.1]).
**The two `suspended` rows in spec/06.** spec/06:257 is the `maxSessionAge` table (SPEC-5a deletes its interrupt sentence) and :280 ("may persist indefinitely") is the clock table that SPEC-5e replaces whole; do not file :280 as a stale site ([spec.38.review-plan-conformance.1]).
**Binderless D1.** D1 admits every replica when no pod binder is wired, so two binderless watchdogs can drive one row; a multi-replica binderless double tail is not filed, because binderless is the single-gateway compose wiring. Refile only with evidence that a multi-replica binderless deployment is supported ([non-spec.45.review-fresh.1], [non-spec.45.review-reliability.1]).

### Open

- **§29.3 interaction-resolution wording.** Pre-existing gap: the base row says the root's coordinator "resolves the pending ... elicitation the pod is blocked on", while for a child's elicitation the blocked pod is the child's and the code resolves it by polling Postgres. It is not this proposal's edit. [spec.30.fix-design-G3.1]
- **§15.1 `awaiting_client_action` "Retries exhausted".** Pre-existing and editorial: the state-table cell misdescribes entry paths (b) to (d); a later round may point it at §7.3 **Entry paths:**. [spec.31.review-edit-sites.1]
- **DOCS-1b echo exception closure.** [redesign1.apply.5] staged the `heartbeat_ack` echo exception on adapter-contract.md, integration-levels.md, and runtime-author-guide/index.md for the OPEN in [reconcile.deliverables.9]; the next non-spec docs round confirms it. [non-spec.42.review-edit-sites.1] had argued those lines were not sites. [redesign1.apply.5]
- **FLAG carried forward.** `recoverDelegationTree` only nudges `DriveResume` and records `full_success` under CODE-4 item 11, so it is a deletion candidate that needs its own proposal; a resumer that no longer holds the session publishes onto the bus with its own per-session sequence while the holder also publishes, which every peer terminal writer shares. [redesign1.apply.5]
- UNVERIFIED: **Binderless double `children_reattached`.** The binderless **Landed** tail can emit it twice against §7.2 "exactly once per parent resume"; CODE-4 item 5.3 accepts this for Compose, and the non-spec loop owns it if filed. [spec.46.review-fresh.1]
- UNVERIFIED: **Item 3.7 report context.** CODE-4 item 3.7 does not state which context the report runs on. `ReportSessionFailure` reads the row on its caller's context (`failure.go`), so a report on a cancelled attempt context fails its read and leaves the row to `sweepResuming`. The outcome is the same after a delay, and the `resumeExit` stays Reported. The one-line fix sends the report on the server-lifetime context. [non-spec.48.review-fresh.1]
- **`ExpireRaisedBy` index cost.** CODE-6 item 19.1 keyed the delete on `idx_interactions_tenant_user`, which reads every interaction the user ever had in the tenant, because resolved rows stay until erasure (`interactionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go` deletes only in `DeleteByUser` and `DeleteByTenant`). The fix adds migration 0185, a partial index on pending elicitations keyed by `(tenant_id, detail->>'originPod')`. Cost: one entry per pending elicitation, kept up by `Put` and `Resolve`; `Resolve` updates stop being HOT-eligible because `phase` and `detail` become indexed; the query must spell `kind` and `phase` as literals so the planner matches the predicate. [mechanism.post-terminal-hook-from-spec-order.1]
- **Snapshot-close bump transaction.** §7.2 snapshot-close step 4 places the `coordination_generation` bump in the transaction that writes the terminal state; the tree makes it a second `Update` (`session_generation.go` `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose`, called from `handleDelete` and `handleTransition`), and D6 **Read authority.** relied on that. The fix moves the bump into `sessionstore.MarkTerminated`. [mechanism.post-terminal-hook-from-spec-order.1]

### Deferred

None unclosed. The last three were closed in [reconcile.deliverables.7], [mechanism.resume-attempts-counter.1], and [redesign1.apply.5].

### [non-spec.48.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the post-commit terminal-write leak with one new **Post-commit tail.** row directly after the Publish row: on **Landed** it re-reads the row with `ReadPrimary` and, on a terminal row, calls `releaseExecutor` with `dispositionForState(row.State)` and skips the remaining rows; every other column skips it — BECAUSE the terminal funnel calls `releaseExecutor` after its write, so whichever of publish and terminal write comes second releases (both releasing is safe: `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds atomically and the second call no-ops) — ALTERNATIVES: (a) test the attempt context's `Err()` after publish instead of reading the row: rejected, the item 3.2/3.4 attempt bounds cancel the same context, so a bound firing after the commit would release a live session's binding; (b) publish before the commit as the shipped `handleResume` did: rejected, contradicts item 4 and SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**; (c) have the terminal funnel reclaim by `PodAssignment`: rejected, session-wide/by-name release violates item 24's generation-scoped release; (d) serialize the funnel and the tail under `slotAccountLocks`: rejected, larger and the lock is already ordered against other paths (item 22, D3 never-nest rule).
FACT: a `running`/`resuming` terminal session is released only through `releaseExecutor`; `terminalReclaimPreRunning` gates on pre-running `fromState`, so nothing reclaims a binding published after the funnel ran — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:447 and :582-586; pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:259-262; coordination.go:308-315 (Sweeper skips terminal rows); failure.go:136-139.
WATCHOUT: a `ReadPrimary` error in the re-check row must continue the Landed column (log it); releasing on a read error tears down a live session. The residual leak then needs a store error concurrent with a terminal write, which is accepted.
WATCHOUT: the binderless sentence after the table counts "the first four rows"; inserting the re-check row at position 3 makes it "the first five rows".
WATCHOUT: `TestResumeDriverPostCommitAndHookOutcomes_spec_7_3` stays one case per column; the terminal case lives in `TestTerminalWriteCancelsSameReplicaResumeAttempt_spec_7_2`. In the column cases the re-check finds a non-terminal row, which the existing published-binding assertion already observes.

### [spec.47.review-performance.1]
FACT: SPEC-5o's tree re-arm touches tree-wide keys plus per-parent keys (`parallel_children:{parent}`, `children_total:{parent}`), so it costs about 3 + 2 x (delegating parents) EXPIREs, and it runs only when a root session enters or leaves `suspended`. That is negligible at Tier 4. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:401-402
FACT: SPEC-1g3's shared-record check adds one lease-record read per renewal. Renewal fires once per lease TTL (`renewBefore` = `expiresAt` - 300 s), so at Tier 4 (100k sessions) this is tens of reads/s. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1265, :1543; spec/16_observability.md:595
WATCHOUT: An idle-suspend write that ERRORS (Postgres failover) does not unseal, because SPEC-5ab unseals only on a checkpoint failure or a condition miss. The session therefore refuses messages, which go to the DLQ, until the next attempt succeeds after the failover. This is deliberate, because the write outcome is ambiguous. Checkpoint-retry amplification is bounded by the 60 s retry record shared with `ReleaseSuspendedPod` (non-spec CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.**). Do not file it as a checkpoint storm. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:825; non-spec-changes.md:430

### [non-spec.50.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: D6 seal race closed by (a) `recordSessionCompleted` holding the session's entry in the existing D6 in-flight record from before `stampTerminatedCondition` until its executor release returns, and (b) `ReleaseTerminalBinding` returning at once on a row whose `TerminatedAt` is zero or whose entry is held — BECAUSE a funnel mark taken after the terminal commit leaves a window: the failure funnel's D6 goroutine waits on `slotAccountLocks` during `accountFailedSlot`, and `transitionToFailed` calls `unlock()` before `recordSessionCompleted`, so D6 can win the wake-up and release before any mark; the `TerminatedAt` stamp is written only by the funnel and only after the claim, so a listing that shows it finds the claim held — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's separate funnel-in-flight set marked inside recordSessionCompleted (racy as above, and a second set beside the D6 record); marking before every terminal `Update` (handleDelete, handleTransition, transitionToFailed, watchdog/orphancleanup writers behind OnSessionTerminal: too many sites); moving the stamp to the end of the funnel (changes the session API's terminal fact timing and the lifecycle signals); D6 sealing before it releases (D6 would write rows on seal timeout, contradicting **Read authority.**, and double-export on the writer replica); gating on row age > sealMaxDuration (5-minute cross-replica latency, clock skew).
FACT: Only `stampTerminatedCondition` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:87-106) assigns `TerminatedAt` in pkg/ and cmd/; terminatedReasonForState covers all four terminal states (usage.go:64-77); pgstore reads terminated_at in its shared column list (pgstore.go:117,184,1132) — EVIDENCE: grep "TerminatedAt = " pkg cmd
FACT: Checkpointer.Seal turns ErrNoBinding into nil, and the tree comment says a session coordinated elsewhere falls back to the latest checkpoint (§7.1); so the cross-replica D6 release without a seal matches the tree's accepted fallback and is not this finding — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:378-401
FACT: The warmpool claim-orphan GC reclaims a claim with no active (non-terminal) session, so a terminal row D6 never reclaims still has its pod drained — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:70-77
WATCHOUT: Publish-site tests (TestStartOnTerminalRowLeavesBindingToReaper, TestTerminalAfterResumeRecheckReclaimed, the create-and-start and delegated-child cases) write a terminal state through a store wrapper that bypasses the funnel; after this fix their write must also set `TerminatedAt`, or `ReleaseTerminalBinding` correctly does nothing and the test fails — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md "Tests that land with CODE-4" entries for those tests

### [non-spec.50.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: no findings in non-spec round 50 under the end-to-end mechanism lens. I re-read CODE-4 item 5 (commit, classifyCommit, Post-commit tail with the terminal re-check row), CODE-5 items 1, 2, and 2a to 2d, and D6 against the tree, and every flow closes. BECAUSE: (1) the publish-site list in D6 is complete; a grep for publishBinding, registerBinding, and podRegistry.Put finds only bind_registration.go, resume_rebind.go, create_and_start.go:530, delegated_child_materialize.go:207, start.go:89, and the readopt closure at coordination_seams.go:262. (2) Every one of those sites stamps a nonzero generation, so the "stamp 0 evicts" branch cannot swallow a live binding. (3) D6's slotAccountLocks wait is ordered against the funnel correctly: transitionToFailed unlocks before recordSessionCompleted (failure.go:441-445), and PodExecutor.Release unbinds atomically (pod.go:257-262), so concurrent D6 and funnel releases make at most one binder call. (4) The binderless "first five rows" ordinal still matches the table (fence, publish, re-check, close, ReleaseUncommitted). ALTERNATIVES: (a) the spec §29.3 terminate/DELETE rows require a forward to the coordinator, while the tree writes the terminal state on any replica (session_transitions.go:20-50), and the D6 tier-4 test and "Why this proposal owes it" rely on that. I rejected this as a finding because the test asserts only the binder-side release, which also holds once a forward exists, and the watchdog lifetime expiry motivates D6 on its own. (b) handleStart's same-replica DELETE between registerBinding and the commit: terminalReclaimPreRunning reclaims the pod by name and skips releaseExecutor (usage.go:430-448), so the binding stays until D6. I rejected this because D6 covers it and the double release fails closed. (c) evictStaleBinding does not close the BindResult adapter connection, so a re-check that only evicts leaks a gRPC client. I rejected this because the Sweeper's peer-takeover evictBinding behaves the same way in the shipped tree, and the case needs a lapsed lease during the commit tail.
FACT: Sweep returns early on a non-NotFound leases.Get error for any non-terminal row (coordination.go:323-326), so one Redis error defers D6 for every later terminal row in that pass. D6 **Accepted gaps.** already names this — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:316-326
FACT: the Sweeper exists only when Redis is wired (cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:248-258). Without Redis, D6 has only the item 5 fast path. That is safe because a no-Redis gateway is single-replica, and handleStart's publish precedes any same-replica terminal write — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:202-258, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:88-117
USEFUL [non-spec.49.review-reliability.1]: its publish-site stamp inventory and the D6/D3 lock-disjointness check let me skip re-deriving the deadlock question.

### [non-spec.50.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: D6's `ReleaseTerminalBinding` goroutine can release a binding while the same replica's terminal funnel is still running the §7.1 seal-and-export on it — BECAUSE `recordSessionCompleted` runs `sealWorkspace` (up to `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`, 300 s default) after the terminal write and before `releaseExecutor`, holding no `slotAccountLocks` (`transitionToFailed` unlocks first; `handleDelete` and the watchdog never take it), so the next Sweeper listing (15 s interval) hands the bound terminal row to D6, which takes the uncontended lock, finds the binding at the row's generation, and releases the pod; the next `Seal` retry then gets `ErrNoBinding`, which `Checkpointer.Seal` maps to nil, so the seal records `success` with no sealed snapshot — ALTERNATIVES: D6 early-return on a Redis `Get` error (already rejected by the performance lens); D6 versus `terminalReclaimPreRunning` double claim release on a `ready` row (speculative, not filed).
FACT: the seal-and-export reads the binding from the replica's pod registry, and a missing binding makes `Seal` return nil. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:396-401, :421-424
FACT: `recordSessionCompleted` runs `sealWorkspace` before the executor release, and `transitionToFailed` calls `unlock()` before `recordSessionCompleted`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:372-391, :445-456; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go (transitionToFailed, `unlock()` then `recordSessionCompleted`)
WATCHOUT: any later change to D6's trigger or lock has to keep D6 off a binding whose terminal funnel is still running on this replica. spec/07:115 requires the export to finish before the pod is released. The remedy belongs in the non-spec staging: a per-session funnel-in-flight mark that `recordSessionCompleted` sets before the seal and clears after its release, which D6 skips. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:115
USEFUL [non-spec.50.review-performance.1]: its confirmation that the D6 trigger sits in the terminal branch ahead of the lease `Get` saved a re-derivation.

### [non-spec.50.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings in non-spec round 50 — BECAUSE a re-read of CODE-4 item 5 (commit, classifyCommit, Post-commit tail), CODE-5 items 1, 2, 2a to 2d, D6, and CODE-4 items 20 to 27 against the tree keeps every release scoped to the attempt's or the binding's own leases (item 24 `ReleaseThrough`, `ReleaseUncommitted` by minted IDs), keeps the readopt pool pinned to `row.PoolRef`, and adds no Kubernetes write, RBAC, egress, or agent-pod apiserver path (grep of both staged files for status.conditions, RBAC, NetworkPolicy, apiserver, egress finds no hit) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) the D6 evict branch on a terminal row leaving the evicted binding's leases recorded, so this replica's renewal worker can keep renewing them until the §4.9 cumulative-extension cap: rejected, because a writer replica without the binding released nothing before 0092 either (`PodExecutor.Release` no-ops without a binding, and `credassign.Client.ReleaseSession` is replica-local, both pre-existing), and the WATCHOUT of [redesign1.apply.6] records the eviction as an accepted D6 gap; (2) runtime `busy` defeating idle suspension: rejected, because before 0092 a runtime could already keep the idle clock fresh with agent_output frames (flags.go:1068 help), and the Postgres active-time cap (CODE-1) still bounds the session.
FACT: The claim-orphan GC that D6's accepted gaps rely on reclaims a claim when no non-terminal session names the pod through `pod_assignment`, so a terminal row's evicted pod is reclaimed — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:27-35, :70-80
FACT: `originPod` in elicitation `detail` is written from the gateway-resolved raising session (`raising.ID`), so CODE-6's `ExpireRaisedBy` keys on a trusted value scoped by tenant and user — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/elicitation.go:221, :405
USEFUL [non-spec.48.review-security.1]: its durable active-time cap fact and its rejection of the Context-ended lease case held without re-derivation.

### [redesign1.apply.7]
DECISION: applied redesign 1 (scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md, the terminal transition, D7) in full: E1 to E36 with E32a, E32b, E34a, and E34b. Every anchor matched exactly once and no edit was skipped. The redesign covered one area, the terminal transition: the new non-spec Design **Terminal transition.** (D7) with `sessionstore.MarkTerminated`, the per-replica terminal claim record `terminalClaims` taken by `BeginTerminal` before the terminal `Update`, the post-terminal hook `AfterTerminalWrite` (cancel the same-replica attempt, wait for a D6 goroutine, seal-and-export, release, `ExpireRaisedBy`), the `funnel`, `hook`, and `none` paths, and the tier-1 `TestTerminalWriterInventory_spec_7_2` as the one list of terminal writers. It also added the D4 **Guarded-write repeat rule.**, renamed the resume driver's in-flight record to the attempt record `resumeAttempts`, and extended SPEC-5p2 expiry to a terminal transition — BECAUSE the D6 seal race kept being re-closed by gating on `TerminatedAt`, which forced every terminal writer outside `recordSessionCompleted` to stamp the row, left an unreclaimed row whenever the funnel's best-effort stamp failed, and duplicated the list of terminal writers across CODE-4 items 2a and 27 — ALTERNATIVES: none taken; OD-1 and OD-2 applied at their defaults.
FACT: deleted from the staging: D6's `TerminatedAt` release gate and its mark-after-commit explanation; the D6 accepted gap for a row whose funnel stamp failed; CODE-4 item 27's writer list and its in-flight-record IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE; CODE-4 item 2a's per-writer `CancelResumeAttempt` caller list; `Deps.ResumeAttempts`; `cascadeToChildren`'s own `releaseExecutor` call; the `reclaimFinalizedPod` call after a `failFinalizing` that returned nil in `finalizePlanParseFailed` and a prepared `finalizeReadyWriteFailed`; the duplicated repeat wording in CODE-4 item 5 step 2 and CODE-6 item 4 step 3; the SPEC-5p2 example of a miss on orphan session reconciliation; the tests `TestTerminalWriteStampsTerminatedAt_spec_7_2`, `TestCancelToolsCancelResumeAttempt_spec_7_2`, and `TestResumeAttemptCancellerWiring_spec_7_2`; and six stale standing Open bullets (OD-R1 to OD-R3, the duplicated `resumeHeldPod` stamp, OD-D6-1, and OD-D6-2). The shipped `stampTerminatedCondition`, `terminatedReasonForState`, `TestTerminalTransitionStampsTerminatedConditionOnRow_spec_7_2`, and `TestTerminalConditionStampIsIdempotent_spec_7_2` stay named in D7 and the Testing section only as deletions the implementor makes. EVIDENCE: git diff of the proposal directory.
FACT: reconciliation beyond the edit list: summary **What changes.** session-server bullet now names the terminal transition; new summary Decision 38 states D7 with the OD-1 and OD-2 defaults; summary `## Open decisions for human to make` carries OD-1 and OD-2; two summary watch-outs added (the `podReclaimed` flag on the finalize paths, and `fromState` from the locked row in the multi-session writers); the summary SPEC-5 and CODE-6 index entries and the elicitation bullet name expiry through a terminal transition. The sweep deleted D7's sentence that `Deps.Terminal` replaces `Deps.ResumeAttempts` and rewrote "terminal pipeline" to "post-terminal hook" in the CODE-6 item 1 test. Checklist dependencies and tiers need no change: S14 already runs tiers 1 and 7a, S5 lands SPEC-5p2, and S15 depends on S14 for hook step 5. Files touched gained `terminal_hook.go`, `seal.go`, `finalize.go`, `orphancleanup.go`, `session_admin.go`, `session_budget.go`, and `reconciler_workers.go` through E28; `terminal_writers_test.go` and `cancel_release_test.go` fall under "and their tests".
FACT: sweep of every staged file for `stampTerminatedCondition`, `terminatedReasonForState`, `TerminatedAt`, `Deps.ResumeAttempts`, "in-flight record", "in-flight mark", "in-flight entry", "terminal funnel", "choke point", "funnel stamp", and "terminal pipeline" finds only the D7 deletion instructions, the `MarkTerminated` behavior and its test, the `cancel_release_test.go` deletion reason, the in-flight entry of `SuspendIdle` and `ReleaseSuspendedPod` (a separate mechanism), and past ledger entries.
OPEN (owner, OD-1): `terminateUserSessions` (§11.4 `full_revoke`) is a `none` writer under default B, so a revoked user's sessions are not sealed and `terminateLocal` keeps its own canceller; option A runs the hook and races the §11.4 `Shutdown`. Recorded in summary Open decisions.
OPEN (owner, OD-2): on the `funnel` path the pod release now precedes the operational event, callback, and `emitTerminalLifecycle` under default A; option B splits the hook to keep today's order. Recorded in summary Open decisions.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the D6 seal-race closure by a `TerminatedAt`-zero gate in `ReleaseTerminalBinding` plus `recordSessionCompleted` holding the D6 in-flight entry from before `stampTerminatedCondition`, with every non-funnel writer stamping through `MarkTerminated`.
  It lost because the gate made reclamation depend on every writer stamping, a failed best-effort funnel stamp left a row D6 never reclaimed, and the writer list was restated in CODE-4 items 2a and 27 and kept drifting.
  Do not gate D6 on a row field again; the terminal claim taken before the `Update` is what orders D6 behind the writer's seal, and the inventory test is the one writer list.

WATCHOUT: Replaced: the per-writer `CancelResumeAttempt` call sites (`recordSessionCompleted`, `expireSession`, `afterFailed`, `cascadeToChildren`, and the MCP cancel tools through `Deps.ResumeAttempts`).
  It lost because each terminal writer carried its own cancel, release, and expiry calls, and a writer added later missed some of them.
  Do not add a post-terminal side effect at a writer; add it as a hook step, and give a writer that skips the hook a `none` row with its reason in `TestTerminalWriterInventory_spec_7_2`.

FACT: Sweep of every staged file except the review log and archive for `OD-2`, `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose`, "returns at once", "separate `Update`", "hook step N", "steps 3 to 6", `userID`, `idx_interactions_tenant_user`, "release precedes", "default A", "option A", "no migration", "two entry points", "ends at step", "snapshot-close bump", and "seal-and-export". The remaining hits are deletion instructions the implementor acts on (the bump function, the CODE-4 item 3.7 call), the 19.1 sentence that names `idx_interactions_tenant_user` as not used, D6 **Decision.**'s "returns at once" (the call stays asynchronous), the renumbered hook steps 3, 6, and 7, and step references inside other items' own numbered lists.

  `MarkTerminated(r, from, now)` bumps when `from` is `resuming` and the state is `cancelled` or `completed`; D6 now relies on a listed terminal row carrying its final generation.

### [spec.48.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no spec-scoped findings in spec round 48 under the performance lens — BECAUSE the only spec delta since round 47 is the SPEC-5p2 item 6 trigger "or a transition to a terminal state"; its cost and failure behaviour (a failed expiry leaves the elicitation pending, which item 6 already states) live entirely in the non-spec CODE-6 item 19 and Design **Terminal transition.** (D7) step 5 — ALTERNATIVES: filing the per-terminal DELETE cost against SPEC-5p2 (rejected: its fix is an index or a narrower key, both non-spec; spec/ never names the `interactions` table).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-6 item 19.1 says pgstore `ExpireRaisedBy` "reads `idx_interactions_tenant_user`". That index is `(tenant_id, user_id)` only (migrations/0031_interactions.up.sql:48-49), and `interactions` has no retention: resolved tool-use rows (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/toolapproval.go:106) and resolved elicitation rows stay until DeleteByUser or DeleteByTenant (pkg/gateway/session/interactionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:218-242). With D7 step 5, the DELETE now runs on every terminal write, which is about 200/s at Tier 3 (the §16.5 session-creation-rate row). For a tenant served by one service-account user, each call scans that user's entire interaction history and filters `kind`, `phase`, and `detail->>'originPod'` on the heap. What is true instead: a partial index such as `(tenant_id, (detail->>'originPod')) WHERE kind = 'elicitation' AND phase = 'pending'` (new migration), or an equivalent bounded key, keeps the per-terminal cost O(pending rows of that session). The non-spec loop owns this.
FACT: the `interactions` table has no TTL or GC sweep. Its only deletes are DeleteByUser, DeleteByTenant, and Resolve's phase change, which keeps the row. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/interactionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:53-257; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:764

DECISION: the §7.3 terminal inbox+DLQ drain MOVES into the D7 hook as a new step 5 (after the release, before the elicitation expiry, which becomes step 6), and `emitTerminalLifecycle` drops its `drainMessagingOnTerminal` call — BECAUSE the hook is the one site every `funnel` and `hook` writer reaches, so one move closes cascadeToChildren, cancelSubtree, and lenny/cancel_session at once without a second drain on the funnel; placing it after step 4 keeps today's funnel order (drain after seal and release) — ALTERNATIVES: add the drain to the hook and keep it in emitTerminalLifecycle (two homes, funnel drains twice); have the three hook writers call emitTerminalLifecycle (also adds SSE/audit/metrics emission nobody reviewed); put the drain before step 2 (would reorder message_expired ahead of seal-and-export).

### [spec.49.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed nothing under the reliability lens in spec round 49 — BECAUSE every terminal path the staged spec text touches agrees with the committed terminal order (§7.1 steps 20 to 24, §29.4 steps 11 to 17, §11.4 steps 6 and 7): SPEC-2d item 2's podless terminate has no pod to seal or release and runs the §7.3 drain; SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** defers to §7.2 snapshot-close (skip seal, attempt-side reclaim, bump in the terminal write, then terminal handling); SPEC-5p2's expiry names no order relative to the release, and D7 hook step 7 puts it after the release as §11.4 orders the dismissal after lease revocation; SPEC-5i **Enqueue racing a terminal transition.** holds however late the drain runs, because the re-read either finds its own entries or the drain took them — ALTERNATIVES: (1) the terminal drain skipped by a crash between the terminal commit and the hook (no reclaimer for DLQ or durable-inbox keys once SPEC-5z item 6 drops the EXPIRE): rejected for this loop, because the EXPIRE deletion is owner answer OD-R2, the DLQ half predates 0092, no staged sentence claims the drain always runs ("an entry leaves the DLQ only through" lists exits), and [non-spec.51.follow-up-fix.1] already carries it as an OPEN; (2) `final_workspace_ref` of a podless `suspended` session terminated to `completed` (§7.1 derive: "`completed` — uses the sealed final workspace"): rejected, podless `suspended` plus terminate predates 0092 (the hold-expiry release), and the release checkpoint keeps the workspace; (3) path (d) `awaiting_client_action` keeps its live pod and a resume claims a fresh one: pre-existing §11.2 behavior, and the old pod falls to the claim-orphan GC once the commit names the new pod.
FACT: §29.4 step 15 groups the terminal mark, the inbox and DLQ drain, the `cascadeOnFailure` evaluation, and the terminal event; steps 16 and 17 are the lease release and the pod release. The current D7 hook order (cancel, D6 wait, seal, drain, signals, release, elicitation expiry) matches it. — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:743-767; non-spec-changes.md D7 **Hook.** steps 1 to 7

### [restructure.tests-as-intents]
DECISION: the owner approved restating every test obligation in non-spec-changes.md `## Testing` as an intent, and the restructure landed on 2026-10-12. Each obligation is one labelled bullet (`[T0.n]` for TEST-0, `[C1.n]` to `[C7.n]` for the tests that land with each code deliverable, and `[T1.n]` for TEST-1) carrying **Intent:** (the behavior the test proves, in one sentence), **Tier:** (the tier numbers of `.claude/rules/test-coverage.md`), **Spec:** (sections cited by heading), and an optional **Discriminates:** clause naming the wrong implementation the test must fail on. The implementor chooses test names, fixtures, helpers, seeding, fakes, assertion mechanics, and file placement; each intent is pinned by at least one test at each named tier, annotated with its `// spec:` sections. Table-driven obligations name the table they iterate (for example every row of the CODE-4 item 25 table) rather than listing rows. Every "Pinned by" citation in the design, the code items, the activity inventory table, RECORDS-1, the summary, and the checklist names intent labels in place of test function names. The terminal writer list that a test body used to hold moved, unchanged, into Design **Terminal transition.** (D7) as the **Terminal writer inventory.** table, and intent C4.49 pins it. BECAUSE about 270 fixture-level test descriptions were re-checked against the tree in every review round, which cost a full pass per round and produced findings about fixtures rather than design defects. ALTERNATIVES: keep fixture-level tests (rejected by the owner for that cost); keep names only for cited tests (rejected because every citation could point at an intent label instead).
WATCHOUT: do not file a finding asking for a test name, a fixture, a helper, a fake configuration, a seeding step, an assertion mechanism, or a test file path. A test-side finding is in scope only when an intent is missing a behavior, failure path, concurrency case, tier, or spec section that the design or a CODE item requires, or when an intent contradicts the design. Add or amend an intent in the same one-bullet form; do not restore fixture-level text. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md `## Testing` preamble.
