  The age check is in `DriveResume` on the step-1 primary row; `resumeHeldPod` admits an over-cap row and `sweepMaxAge` expires it from `running` (Edge cases **Over-cap row entering `running`.**). Do not restore the refusal unless OD-TT1 is answered B.

WATCHOUT: Replaced: `ReleaseUncommitted` in `release.go` as the credential-free teardown of a split `Release` and `ReleaseSlot`, releasing on every `commitNotLanded` exit.
  It lost because after a store error the commit may have landed and the pod may have passed to an adopter, so an unconditional release could free another owner's pod, slot, or leases, and the split duplicated `failResume`'s teardown.
  `ReleaseUncommitted` shares `failResume`'s teardown and, with `requireReclaimed`, releases nothing unless the reclaim answers `RECLAIMED`; the leases then wait for a later pod release or expiry. Do not release on any other reply.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5.3; spec-changes.md SPEC-1b, SPEC-1g; Edge cases **Credential leases with no binding releaser.**
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the commit classification that counted a miss after a store error as landed when `R` showed `podHeld` at `G` on the result's sandbox.
  It lost because a later incarnation on a concurrent pool's shared pod, or a terminal row, matches that test without this commit having landed or rules it out wrongly.
  A miss counts as landed only on the commit's own evidence: the result's sandbox and a `RecoveryGeneration` above the step-1 row (`b.recoveryGen`), with `podHeld(R)` or a terminal `R`. Do not drop the `RecoveryGeneration` conjunct.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5.3 classification table; intent C4.14
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the `full_revoke` cancel and drain on `podTerminateFanOut` (a `drainer` field, `TerminateUserSessions` draining, `terminateLocal` cancelling).
  It lost because the fan-out is constructed only where a pod registry is wired and also runs on peers through propagation, while the §11.4 cancel and drain belong right after each write on the writing replica.
  `terminateUserSessions` calls the session server's `AfterRevokeWrite` right after each `cancelled` write through the `admin.Router` field `revoked`; `user_revocation.go` is not touched. Do not move the cancel or drain back onto the fan-out.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 2a, D7 **Seams.**; intents C4.59 to C4.61
WATCHOUT: Replaced: tree recovery that selected nodes by absence from the local pod registry (`nodeNeedsRecovery`) and a `ReattachNode` that returned nil for any non-`resume_pending` node or right after calling `DriveResume`.
  It lost because `podsession.Registry` holds only this replica's bindings, so a peer-bound node read as orphaned (TT-48), and `recovery.Recover` records any nil return as `OutcomeRecovered`.
  Selection is by row state `resume_pending`; every selected node of a level is driven before any wait, and `ReattachNode` polls the primary row until the restore's outcome under its node deadline. Do not reintroduce registry-based selection or an early nil return.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 11; intents C4.20 and T1.36; RECORDS-1 item 6c
WATCHOUT: Replaced: `fenceResumedPod` fencing through `Fence`, which retries at a re-read generation.
  It lost because a takeover racing the commit raised the generation, and a retry at the re-read value stamped the binding with a generation the pod never accepted at `G`.
  `fenceResumedPod` passes `G` to the new `FenceAt`, which relinquishes on a stale rejection; the readopt keeps `Fence`. Do not retry the resume fence at a re-read value.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5 step 4; intent C4.63
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the edge case **Concurrent-pool counter after an ambiguous commit.** and RECORDS-1 items 7(c) and 7(k).
  The edge case lost because `ReleaseUncommitted` no longer releases a slot without a `RECLAIMED` reply; 7(c) and 7(k) lost because BUILD-GAPS already carries F-8.10.18, F-10.1.34, and F-11.3.40, and RECORDS-1 refers to an existing finding by its F-id and files none again.
  The remaining slot and claim departures are **Uncommitted replacement pod disposition.** and **Terminal row with no binding at its generation.** (owner P1). Do not refile the deleted letters; the other letters keep their names so cross-references resolve.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md RECORDS-1 lead and item 7; Edge cases
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the §7.2 snapshot-close bump in `MarkTerminated` only for `cancelled` or `completed` from `resuming`.
  It lost because SPEC-1b treats every terminal write from `resuming` as a snapshot close (0092-A17 in `scratchpad/terminal-transition/distinct-defects.md`), so a `failed` or `expired` write from `resuming` skipped the bump that SPEC-1b requires.
  `MarkTerminated` bumps `CoordinationGeneration` by one for every terminal state written from `resuming` (D7 **Mark.**, intent C4.48). Do not narrow the condition to two states again.
  EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md D7 **Mark.**; intent C4.48

### [spec.52.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: returned no findings in spec round 52 under the end-to-end mechanism lens — BECAUSE the only uncommitted spec-staging delta is still SPEC-5b step 2's retire clause, and it agrees with the stream-failure definition at spec/28_communication-channels.md:256 (a stream the gateway did not end, ending DEADLINE_EXCEEDED or INTERNAL), with spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:532, and with SPEC-5ab's "`session_sealed` is not a stream failure" (FAILED_PRECONDITION is outside the :256 definition) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the non-spec "release unbinds before endConn" gap as a spec contradiction (rejected: a release that evicts the conn is a gateway-ended stream, so :256 does not call it a stream failure).
FACT: the unconfirmed-reclaim lease rule closes end to end: an attempt whose commit try hit a store error keeps leases recorded at its entry generation G; every later restore binds at G' > G and its pod release returns leases at or below G', and a landed-then-overtaken commit's reclaim carries G and is rejected stale by a pod an adopter fenced at G+1, which is why the unconfirmed branch releases nothing. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, SPEC-1g; non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5 (`ReleaseUncommitted` requireReclaimed, `FenceAt`).
USEFUL [spec.51.review-fresh.1]: its note that the delta is one clause and that spec/ is unchanged since 2026-10-06 let this round spend its budget on whole-flow traces rather than anchor re-verification.
USEFUL [spec.24.review-single-source.1]: the Traps **Pre-running DLQ TTL.** entry stopped a refile of the dead-letter Pre-running row against SPEC-5i's recovering-only enforcement sentence.

### [spec.52.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed nothing under the reliability lens in spec round 52 — BECAUSE the only spec-staging delta since round 50 is SPEC-5b step 2's retire clause (already checked in [spec.51.review-fresh.1]), and a whole re-read of SPEC-1 to SPEC-6 traced through crash, restart, and store failover finds every candidate either bounded by a stated backstop or already settled: the held-message interleavings of a non-coordinator holder against an interrupt release (every ordering of enqueue, clear write, releaser evaluation, and the holder's guarded resume_pending write ends resumed, with the leader re-check as backstop); a stale driver racing a takeover CAS (generation CAS settles both orders); a retried commit after a store error (witness lives in non-spec classifyCommit, Settled **Commit-miss classification.**); unconfirmed-reclaim leases kept renewed until a later pod release or session end (bounded, SPEC-1g says "returned by a later release ... or it expires"); a lost SealSession answer leaving a seal (SPEC-5ab accepts it, Settled **Not-sealed keeps the seal.**) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) Redis-only 7-day DLQ custody for podless `suspended` against §12.4 "ephemeral" (rejected: Traps **Refuted: store-outage degradation.** still stands for message loss); (2) a replica that stopped renewing a podless lease and then sees `resume_pending` (rejected: the Sweeper mirror release drops the lease within one sweep, and a lapse mid-restore only costs a post-commit takeover); (3) retryCount never reset on success, so a long-lived session resumed from podless `suspended` may get one attempt (rejected: pre-existing §7.3 accounting).
FACT: claimOrphanTimeout defaults to 5 minutes, equal to the 300 s `resuming` watchdog, so SPEC-1b's accepted "restore still running at that age loses its pod" and the watchdog bound coincide — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241
FACT: §6.2 graceful-release step 3 (checkpoint failure) retries every 60 s and holds the pod; its exit list predates SPEC-5g's lifetime expiry and is in **Not filed, do not refile.** — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:230
USEFUL [Traps "Refuted: store-outage degradation"]: covered the Redis DLQ custody candidate again without re-deriving it.
